(\mathcal{B},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
, then
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}
;

(ii)

If
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},J}\leq 1
for all
I\subseteq J\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I}}
.

Proof.

We use induction on
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert
to show that
(i)
and
(ii)
hold.
If
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert=0
, then
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}=1=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}
and hence
(i)
and
(ii)
are true.

Let
\ell\geq 1
and suppose that both statements hold if
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert\leq\ell-1
.
Suppose that
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert=\ell
.
First, suppose that there is an isolated vertex
v\notin I
in
\mathcal{A}
.
If
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}\geq 1
for all
(\mathcal{B},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
, using the induction hypothesis, we obtain

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}=(n-\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert+1)\cdot\Phi_{\mathcal{A}-\mathopen{\{}v\mathclose{\}},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A}-\mathopen{\{}v\mathclose{\}},I}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I},

so
(i)
holds.
Furthermore, we have
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},V_{\mathcal{A}}\setminus\mathopen{\{}v\mathclose{\}}}=n>1
, so
(ii)
is vacuously true.

Hence, now suppose that there is no isolated vertex
v\notin I
in
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
I\subseteq U\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\rho_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}
is maximal and subject to this, that
\lvert U\rvert
is minimal.
Then,
(\mathcal{A}[U],I)
is strictly balanced.
Furthermore, since there are no isolated vertices
v\notin I
in
\mathcal{A}
, we have
\rho_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}\geq\rho_{\mathcal{A},I}>0
by choice of
U
and hence
U\neq I
.
Note that

(7.7)

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}=\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}.

To obtain
(i)
and
(ii)
, we use the strict balancedness of
(\mathcal{A}[U],I)
to bound
\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}
and the induction hypothesis to bound
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}
for all
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}^{\sim}
.

In more detail, for
(i)
we argue as follows.
Suppose that
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}\geq 1
for all
(\mathcal{B},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
.
For all
(\mathcal{B},U)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},U)
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{A}[U]
, we have
\rho_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime},I}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}
by choice of
U
.
Thus, since
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}[U]=\mathcal{A}[U]
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}[I]=\mathcal{A}[I]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},U}

\displaystyle=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime},U}=n^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\rvert-\lvert U\rvert}\hat{p}^{\lvert\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{B}^{\prime}[I]\rvert-(\lvert\mathcal{A}[U]\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}[I]\rvert)}=n^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\rvert-\lvert U\rvert}\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime},I}(\lvert V_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)-\rho_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}(\lvert U\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)}

\displaystyle\geq n^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\rvert-\lvert U\rvert}\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime},I}(\lvert V_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\rvert-\lvert U\rvert)}=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime},I}^{(\lvert V_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\rvert-\lvert U\rvert)/(\lvert V_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)}\geq 1.

Hence, for all
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}^{\sim}
, by induction hypothesis,

(7.8)

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},U}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},U}.

The template
(\mathcal{A}[U],I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
is strictly balanced.
Furthermore, since we suppose that
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}\geq 1
for all
(\mathcal{B},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
, we have
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}\geq 1
.
Thus, Lemma
7.14
entails

(7.9)

\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}.

Combining (
7.8
) and (
7.9
) with (
7.7
), we obtain

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}\cdot(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},U}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},U}.

Hence, employing Observation
7.1
as well as Lemma
7.2
yields

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}

and thus shows that
(i)
holds.
Recall that, as mentioned above, when we use the fact expressed in Lemma
7.2
, we will not always explicitly reference this lemma.

Let us turn to
(ii)
.
Now, no longer suppose that necessarily
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}\geq 1
for all
(\mathcal{B},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
and instead suppose that
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},J}\leq 1
for all
I\subseteq J\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Then, in particular
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},J}\leq 1
for all
U\subseteq J\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Hence, for all
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}^{\sim}
, by induction hypothesis,

(7.10)

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},U}}.

The template
(\mathcal{A}[U],I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
is strictly balanced.
Furthermore, since we suppose that
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},J}\leq 1
for all
I\subseteq J\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
, so in particular
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq 1
, and since
\rho_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}
by choice of
U
, we obtain

\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}\leq n^{\lvert U\rvert-\lvert I\rvert}\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{A},I}(\lvert U\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)}=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}^{(\lvert U\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)/(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)}\leq 1.

Hence, Lemma
7.14
entails

(7.11)

\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}}.

Similarly as above, combining (
7.10
) and (
7.11
) with (
7.7
) and employing Observation
7.1
yields

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}}\cdot(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},U}}\leq(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I}}

and hence shows that
(ii)
holds.
∎

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
i\geq 0
and
\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i<\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\mathclose{\}}
.
Suppose that
(\mathcal{A},I)
is a template with
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert\leq 1/\varepsilon^{4}
, let
I\subsetneq J\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
and from all subtemplates
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
with
J\subseteq V_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
, choose
(\mathcal{B},I)
such that
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}
is minimal.
Let
\psi\colon J\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
Then,
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},J}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}/\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}
.

Proof.

Since
\lvert\mathcal{A}[V_{\mathcal{B}}]\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}[I]\rvert\geq\lvert\mathcal{B}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{B}[I]\rvert
entails
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}[V_{\mathcal{B}}],I}\leq\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}
, we may assume that
\mathcal{B}
is an induced subgraph of
\mathcal{A}
.
Indeed, by choice of
\mathcal{B}
, we obtain
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}[V_{\mathcal{B}}],I}=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}
, so we may replace
\mathcal{B}
with
\mathcal{A}[V_{\mathcal{B}}]
since the statement we wish to obtain only depends on
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}
.
Note that

(7.12)

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}=\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{B},\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}.

We use Lemma
7.15
to bound
\Phi_{\mathcal{B},\psi}
and
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}
for all
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{B},\psi}
.

In more detail, we argue as follows.
Let
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{B},\psi}
and consider a subtemplate
(\mathcal{C},V_{\mathcal{B}})\subseteq(\mathcal{A},V_{\mathcal{B}})
.
Then, for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{C}+\mathcal{A}[V_{\mathcal{B}}]
, we have
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}\leq\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime},I}
by choice of
(\mathcal{B},I)
and hence

\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C},V_{\mathcal{B}}}=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime},V_{\mathcal{B}}}=\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime},I}}{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}}\geq 1.

Thus, Lemma
7.15

(i)
entails

(7.13)

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},V_{\mathcal{B}}}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},V_{\mathcal{B}}}.

Next, in order so bound
\Phi_{\mathcal{B},\psi}
, suppose that
J\subseteq J^{\prime}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{B}}
.
Then,
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}\leq\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B}[J^{\prime}],I}
by choice of
(\mathcal{B},I)
and hence

\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},J^{\prime}}=\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}}{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B}[J^{\prime}],I}}\leq 1.

Thus, Lemma
7.15

(ii)
entails

(7.14)

\Phi_{\mathcal{B},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{B},J}}.

Since
\mathcal{B}
is an induced subgraph of
\mathcal{A}
, combining (
7.13
) and (
7.14
) with (
7.12
) and employing Observation
7.1
yields

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{B},J}}\cdot(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},V_{\mathcal{B}}}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},V_{\mathcal{B}}}\leq(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},J}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},V_{\mathcal{B}}}=(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},J}}\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}}{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.17
.

Let
i\geq 0
and
\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i<\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\mathclose{\}}
.
Suppose that
(\mathcal{A},I)
is a
k
-template with
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert\leq 1/\varepsilon^{4}
and let
\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
. Let
e\in\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{A}[I]
and from all subtemplates
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
with
e\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
, choose
(\mathcal{B},I)
such that
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}
is minimal.
Then,

\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(e)\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(i+1)\mathclose{\}}\rvert\leq_{\mathcal{X}}2k!\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert(\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I\cup e}}\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}}{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}}.

Proof.

Note that

\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(e)\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(i+1)\mathclose{\}}\rvert\leq\sum_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(i+1)}\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(e)=f\mathclose{\}}\rvert,

so it suffices to obtain

\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(e)=f\mathclose{\}}\rvert\leq_{\mathcal{X}}2k!\,(\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I\cup e}}\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}}{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}}.

for all
f\in\mathcal{H}(0)
.
This is a consequence of Lemma
7.16

In detail, we argue as follows.
Fix
f\in\mathcal{H}(0)
.
We have

(7.15)

\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(e)=f\mathclose{\}}\rvert\leq\sum_{\psi^{\prime}\colon I\cup e\hookrightarrow\psi(I)\cup f\colon\psi^{\prime}|_{I}=\psi}\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi^{\prime}}.

For
\psi^{\prime}\colon I\cup e\hookrightarrow\psi(I)\cup f
, Lemma
7.16
entails

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi^{\prime}}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I\cup e}}\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}}{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}}\leq 2(\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I\cup e}}\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}}{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}}.

Combining this upper bound with (
7.15
) completes the proof.
∎

7.4.
Degrees

The numbers of embeddings of the templates
(\mathcal{F},f)
where
f\in\mathcal{F}
play a special role since they are closely related to the degrees in
\mathcal{H}^{*}
.

Lemma 7.18
.

Let
i\geq 0
and
e\in\mathcal{H}
.
Then,

d_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}(e)=\frac{\sum_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\psi\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi}}{\aut(\mathcal{F})}.

Proof.

Let
\psi_{0}\colon\emptyset\to V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
We have

d_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}(e)=\frac{\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{F},\psi_{0}}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}e\in\varphi(\mathcal{F})\mathclose{\}}\rvert}{\aut(\mathcal{F})}=\frac{\sum_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\psi\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi}}{\aut(\mathcal{F})},

which completes the proof.

∎

Lemma 7.19
.

Let
0\leq i\leq i^{\star}
and
\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i<\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\mathclose{\}}
.
Consider distinct
e_{1},e_{2}\in\mathcal{H}
and
f\in\mathcal{F}
.
Then,
d_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}(e_{1},e_{2})\leq_{\mathcal{X}}\zeta^{2+\varepsilon^{2}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}
.

Proof.

We have

d_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}(e_{1},e_{2})\leq\sum_{f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\psi\colon f_{1}\cup f_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e_{1}\cup e_{2}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi}.

Fix distinct
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}
,
J:=f_{1}\cup f_{2}
and
\psi\colon J\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e_{1}\cup e_{2}
.
We obtain a suitable upper bound for
\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi}
from Lemma
7.16
as follows.

Since
(\mathcal{F},f_{1})
is balanced, for all
(\mathcal{A},f_{1})\subseteq(\mathcal{F},f_{1})
with
J\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
, we have
\rho_{\mathcal{A},f_{1}}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
and hence using Lemma
7.5
, we obtain

\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},f_{1}}=(n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{A},f_{1}}})^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-k}\geq(n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-k}\geq n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}}.

Thus, Lemma
7.16
together with Lemma
7.8
entails

\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}n^{\varepsilon^{2}}\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f_{1}}}{n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}}}=n^{-\varepsilon^{2}}\zeta^{2}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f_{1}}\leq n^{-\varepsilon^{2}/2}\frac{\zeta^{2}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f_{1}}}{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert^{2}(2k)!}\leq\frac{\zeta^{2+\varepsilon^{2}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f_{1}}}{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert^{2}(2k)!},

which completes the proof.
∎

7.5.
Concentration of key quantities

Overall our proof relies on showing that key quantities that are crucial for our precise analysis of the process are typically concentrated around a deterministic trajectory.
Establishing concentration for any of these quantities relies on the assumption that the other key quantities behave as expected.
More specifically, for certain collections of key quantities, we show that it is unlikely that a key quantity from this collection is the first among all key quantities to significantly deviate from its corresponding trajectory as long as only steps
0\leq i\leq i^{\star}
are considered.
Before we turn to the core of our argument that allows us to analyze the removal process in our very general setting, we end this section with Lemma
7.20
below that provides such statements for three collections of key quantities that correspond to stopping times defined above.
Recall that as defined (
7.1
), the stopping time in
\tilde{\tau}^{\star}
is the minimum of the four stopping times introduced in Section
7
.

Lemma 7.20
.

(i)

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\tau_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}\leq\tilde{\tau}^{\star}\wedge i^{\star}\mathclose{]}\leq\exp(-n^{\varepsilon^{2}})
.

(ii)

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\leq\tilde{\tau}^{\star}\wedge i^{\star}\mathclose{]}\leq\exp(-n^{\delta^{2}})
.

(iii)

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\leq\tilde{\tau}^{\star}\wedge i^{\star}\mathclose{]}\leq\exp(-(\log n)^{3/2})
.

The three parts of Lemma
7.20
can be proven by standard applications of the critical interval method.
Essentially, the argumentation for the analogous statements in the triangle case, see
[
6
, Sections 2 and 3]
, can be adapted to the more general setting without encountering any major obstacles.
We remark that for Lemma
7.20

(i)
, similarly as in
[
6
]
, it is crucial to exploit that if for some
i\geq 0
, the hypergraph
\mathcal{H}^{*}
is approximately vertex-regular and has negligible
2
-degrees, we may approximate

\mathbb{E}_{i}\mathopen{[}\Delta H^{*}\mathclose{]}\approx-\frac{1}{H^{*}}\sum_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{*}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}d_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}(e)=-\frac{1}{H^{*}}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{H}^{*}}d_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}(e)^{2}\approx-\frac{1}{H^{*}}\frac{\lparen\sum_{e\in\mathcal{H}^{*}}d_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}(e)\rparen^{2}}{H}=-\frac{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert^{2}H^{*}}{H}.

Formally, one may rely on the following simple Lemma from
[
3
]
which we also apply further below.

Lemma 7.21

(
[
3
, Lemma 3.1]
)
.

Let
a,a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}
and
b,b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}
such that
\lvert a_{i}-a\rvert\leq\alpha
and
\lvert b_{i}-b\rvert\leq\beta
for all
i,j\in[n]
.
Then,

\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}a_{i}b_{i}=\frac{1}{n}\Big\lparen\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}a_{i}\Big\rparen\Big\lparen\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}b_{i}\Big\rparen\pm 2\alpha\beta n.

Proof.

Note that

\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}a_{i}b_{i}-\frac{1}{n}\Big\lparen\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}a_{i}\Big\rparen\Big\lparen\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}b_{i}\Big\rparen=\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}(a_{i}-a)(b_{i}-b)-\frac{1}{n}\Big\lparen\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}(a_{i}-a)\Big\rparen\Big\lparen\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}(b_{i}-b)\Big\rparen.

By the triangle inequality, we have

\Big\lvert\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}(a_{i}-a)(b_{i}-b)\Big\rvert\leq\alpha\beta n\quad\text{and}\quad\Big\lvert\Big\lparen\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}(a_{i}-a)\Big\rparen\Big\lparen\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}(b_{i}-b)\Big\rparen\Big\rvert\leq\alpha\beta n^{2},

so the statement follows.
∎

Furthermore, when adapting the arguments from the triangle case, Lemma
7.19
replaces the trivial upper bound on the
2
-degrees in
\mathcal{H}^{*}
(given two edges, there is at most one triangle containing both).
For completeness, we provide proofs for the three parts of Lemma
7.20
in Appendices
A
–
C
.

8.
Chains

Our precise analysis of the hypergraph removal process crucially relies on precise estimates for the random variables
\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi}
where
\psi\colon f\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
for some
f\in\mathcal{F}
that essentially correspond to the degrees in the random
\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert
-graph
\mathcal{H}^{*}
(see Lemma
7.18
).
More precisely, Lemma
7.20
provides estimates for key quantities at step
i
that hold with high probability only while
i<\tau_{\mathscr{F}}
.
To complete our argument based on stopping times as outlined at the end of Section
6
, we need to show that this typically holds if
i\leq i^{\star}
provided that the key quantities analyzed in these previous sections behaved as expected up to this step.

The desire to control these numbers of embeddings motivates the definition of a collection
\mathfrak{C}
of carefully chosen templates that includes the templates
(\mathcal{F},f)\in\mathscr{F}
.
Before providing formal definitions of the concepts involved in the definitions of these templates in Section
8.1
, we first give some motivation and intuition where we omit some details.

We obtain the aforementioned templates from structures that we call
chains
and remark that in
[
6
]
, substructures playing a similar role for the special case where
\mathcal{F}
is a triangle are called
ladders
.
Similarly as in
[
6
]
, our choice of chains is based on the following idea.
For a chain template
(\mathcal{C},I)
,
\psi\colon I\rightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
and
e\in\mathcal{C}\setminus\mathcal{C}[I]
, to estimate the number of embeddings
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{C},\psi}
lost due to
\varphi(e)\notin\mathcal{H}(i+1)
, for an edge
f\in\mathcal{F}
and a bijection
\beta\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e
, we are interested in the number

(8.1)

\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{C},\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}

Simply obtaining an estimate for this number based on our estimates for
\Phi_{\mathcal{C},\psi}
and
\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi^{\prime}}
where
\psi^{\prime}\colon f\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
would lead to an undesirable accumulation of errors.
Instead, to achieve more precision that in the end allows us to closely follow the evolution of key quantities for a sufficient number of steps, the initial idea might be to include a chain in our collection
\mathfrak{C}
that provides a template
(\mathcal{C}_{+},I)
where
\mathcal{C}_{+}
is, in an intuitive sense, an
extension
of
\mathcal{C}
obtained from
\mathcal{C}
by gluing a copy
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{F}
onto
\mathcal{C}
such that for all
v\in f
, the vertex
v
is identified with
\beta(v)
while no other vertices outside
e
and
f
are identified with one another.
Then, we could simply consider
\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{+},\psi}
.
However, iterating this unrestricted extension approach yields a growing collection of chains that quickly becomes uncontrollable.
To prevent this, we introduce another chain transformation that we call
reduction
that is meant to counterbalance the extension steps by potentially removing vertices from chains that grow due to extension such that in the end, up to being copies of one another, we only need a finite collection of chains.
In particular, we are interested in a transformation
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathcal{C}_{+}
that we call
branching
of
\mathcal{C}
and that is obtained by combining an extension operation with a reduction operation. Formally, we define
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
to be a suitable induced subgraph of
\mathcal{C}_{+}
.
If for the vertex set
V^{\prime\prime}
of the branching
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
, the embeddings of the template
(\mathcal{C}_{+},V^{\prime\prime})
can be controlled based on our estimates for embeddings of balanced templates, then it could appear sensible to approximate the number in (
8.1
) as

(8.2)

\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{C},\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}\approx\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime},\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{+},\varphi}.

Recall that our motivation was to analyze the one-step changes of
\Phi_{\mathcal{C},\psi}
and that our goal is to exploit the self-correcting behavior of this number of embeddings in the following sense: If there are more embeddings than expected, then it is more likely that embeddings get destroyed hence providing a self-correcting drift (and similarly if there are fewer embeddings than expected).
With the expression in (
8.2
) based only on the branching, this is hard to exploit directly since there is no explicit dependence on
\Phi_{\mathcal{C},\psi}
.
To remedy this, we define another chain, which we call
support
, that is obtained from the branching through another transformation, which we call
truncation
.
During truncation, we remove what remains of the vertices that were added when the copy
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
was glued onto
\mathcal{C}
and we choose the branching such that this truncation can be undone by again gluing the copy
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
onto the support.
This yields an induced subgraph
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{C}
which only depends on
e
and the original chain.
We ensure that for the vertex set
V^{\prime}
of the support, the embeddings of the template
(\mathcal{C},V^{\prime})
can be controlled based on our estimates for embeddings of balanced templates.
Then, Lemma
7.21
allows us approximate the number in (
8.1
) as

(8.3)

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{C},\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\psi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi^{\prime}\circ\beta}\Phi_{\mathcal{C},\psi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\big\lparen\sum_{\psi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi^{\prime}\circ\beta}\big\rparen\big\lparen\sum_{\psi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathcal{C},\psi^{\prime}}\big\rparen}{\Phi_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\psi}}\approx\frac{\Phi_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime},\psi}}{\Phi_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{C},\psi}.

The choice for our collection
\mathfrak{C}
of chains is motivated by the fact that for such an argument,
\mathfrak{C}
needs to be closed under taking branchings and supports of chains contained in
\mathfrak{C}
.

In Section
8.1
, we formally define the terms
chain
,
extension
,
truncation
,
reduction
,
branching
and
support
and we fix our collection
\mathfrak{C}
.
In Section
8.2
, we turn the motivation outlined here into formal arguments to obtain a version of (
8.3
) with quantified errors.
Our arguments that rely on the self-correcting behavior require a careful choice of error terms as well as a consideration of chains in groups that we call
branching families
to exploit symmetry that we discuss in Section
9.1
.
While we defer the analysis of branching families to Section
9
, we define them in Section
8.3
and subsequently use them in a supermartingale argument based on the insight from Section
8.2
that ensures that the embeddings of chains are typically concentrated as desired.

8.1.
Formal definition

Consider a sequence
A=\mathcal{A}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{A}_{\ell}
of
k
-graphs where
\ell\geq 0
and for
0\leq i\leq\ell
define
q_{i}:=1+\sum_{1\leq j\leq i}(\lvert\mathcal{A}_{j}\rvert-1)
.
We say that
A
is a
loose path
starting at a
k
-set
I
if there exists an ordering
e_{1},\ldots,e_{q_{\ell}}
of
\mathcal{A}_{1}+\ldots+\mathcal{A}_{\ell}
such that
e_{1}=I
and such that
\mathcal{A}_{i}=\mathopen{\{}e_{q_{i-1}},\ldots,e_{q_{i}}\mathclose{\}}
for all
1\leq i\leq\ell
.
We call
A

vertex-separated
if
V_{\mathcal{A}_{1}+\ldots+\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{A}_{i}+\ldots+\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}=e_{q_{i-1}}
for all
2\leq i\leq\ell
.

A triple
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)
where
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
with
\ell\geq 0
is a
chain
if
F
is the empty sequence and
V=I
is a
k
-set or if
F
is a vertex-separated loose path of copies of
\mathcal{F}
starting at
I
such that
I\subseteq V\subseteq V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}+\ldots+\mathcal{F}_{\ell}}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{F}}\cup\mathbb{N}
.
The choice of
\mathbb{N}
here is essentially arbitrary and only serves to provide some infinite set of potential vertices, which is convenient when we want to consider the set of all chains.
The
chain template
given by
\mathfrak{c}
is the template
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},I)
where
\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}
is the
k
-graph with vertex set
I
and edge set
\mathopen{\{}I\mathclose{\}}
if
\ell=0
and where
\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}=(\mathcal{F}_{1}+\ldots+\mathcal{F}_{\ell})[V]
otherwise.

We now formally define the three basic transformations of chains mentioned in the beginning of this section:
extension
,
truncation
and
reduction
.

For all
\beta\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e
where
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I]
such that
e\notin\mathcal{F}_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq\ell-1
, fix an arbitrary copy
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}
of
\mathcal{F}
with vertex set
V_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}\subseteq e\cup\mathbb{N}
such that the following holds

(i)

e\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}
;

(ii)

V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}+\ldots+\mathcal{F}_{\ell}}\cap V_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}=e
;

(iii)

there exists a bijection
\beta^{\prime}\colon V_{\mathcal{F}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}V_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}
with
\beta^{\prime}(f^{\prime})\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}
for all
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
and
\beta^{\prime}|_{f}=\beta
;

(iv)

V_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta_{1}}\cap V_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta_{2}}=e
for all distinct
\beta_{1}\colon f_{1}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e
and
\beta_{2}\colon f_{2}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e
with
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}
.

The
\beta
-extension
of
\mathfrak{c}
is the chain
\mathfrak{c}|\beta:=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)
where

F^{\prime}:=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}\quad\text{and}\quad V^{\prime}:=V\cup V_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}.

For
0\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
, the
\ell^{\prime}
-truncation
of
\mathfrak{c}
is the chain
\mathfrak{c}|\ell^{\prime}:=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)
where
F^{\prime}
is the empty sequence and
V^{\prime}=I
if
\ell^{\prime}=0
and where

F^{\prime}:=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell^{\prime}}\quad\text{and}\quad V^{\prime}:=V\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}+\ldots+\mathcal{F}_{\ell^{\prime}}}

otherwise.
For convenience, we set
\mathfrak{c}|\mathord{-}:=\mathfrak{c}|\ell-1
if
\ell\geq 1
.

If
\ell=0
, let
W_{\mathfrak{c}}:=V
.
If
\ell\geq 1
, then, among the vertex sets
W
with
(V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cup V_{\mathcal{F}_{\ell}})\cap V\subseteq W\subsetneq V
and
\rho_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},W}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}
, choose
W_{\mathfrak{c}}
such that
\lvert W_{\mathfrak{c}}\rvert
is maximal if such a vertex set exists and choose
W_{\mathfrak{c}}=V
otherwise.
The
reduction
of
\mathfrak{c}
is the chain
\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}
inductively defined as follows.
If
W_{\mathfrak{c}}=V
, then
\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}:=\mathfrak{c}
.
If
W_{\mathfrak{c}}\neq V
, then
\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}:=(F,W_{\mathfrak{c}},I)|\text{r}
.
It is easy to see that this indeed provides a well-defined reduction for all chains.
Crucially, Lemma
7.3
guarantees that each reduction step corresponds to a strictly balanced extension in the sense that if
W_{\mathfrak{c}}\neq V
, then
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},W_{\mathfrak{c}})
is strictly balanced.

Figure 1.
A
3
-uniform chain template
(\mathcal{C},I)
for the special case where
\mathcal{F}=K^{(3)}_{4}
.
The chain template is given by a chain
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)
where
I=\mathopen{\{}1,2\mathclose{\}}
and where
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{50}
is a sequence of
50
copies of
\mathcal{F}
whose vertices are elements of
\mathopen{\{}1,\ldots,54\mathclose{\}}
.
The vertex sets of these copies are listed below the visualization of the chain template and for each copy
\mathcal{F}_{i}
with
1\leq i\leq 49
, the unique vertex of
\mathcal{F}_{i}
that is not a vertex of
\mathcal{F}_{i+1}
underlined.
Instead of drawing the edges of
\mathcal{C}
, we instead draw edges of the links of selected colored, that is red, green blue or orange, vertices.
Here, the
link
of a vertex
u\in V_{\mathcal{C}}
is the
2
-graph with vertex set
V_{\mathcal{C}}
where
\mathopen{\{}v,w\mathclose{\}}
is an edge if
\mathopen{\{}u,v,w\mathclose{\}}\in\mathcal{C}
.
To distinguish more clearly between edges of
\mathcal{C}
and edges of the links, here we call edges of
\mathcal{C}

faces
.
For every face
f\in\mathcal{C}
, there exists a colored vertex
v\in f
such that
f\setminus\mathopen{\{}v\mathclose{\}}
is one of the edges of the link of
v
that is drawn in the same color as
v
.
Hence, for a vertex
u
, incident faces are represented either by incident edges of a link of another vertex or as edges that have the same color as
u
.
Not all edges of the link of a colored vertex are drawn.
Every face is represented by exactly one drawn edge, so in particular, the number of faces is the number of drawn edges.
Exactly two vertices of every copy in
F
are colored.
Furthermore, the drawn edges are selected such that every copy
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
in
F
corresponds to a monochromatic triangle together with a vertex of the same color in the following sense: the vertex together with the vertices of the triangle forms the vertex set of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and the edges of the triangle together with an edge that has the same color as the unique colored vertex of the triangle represent the faces of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
.
Selected copies in
F
are highlighted using a colored background.

Suppose that
\varepsilon^{2}=1/10
.
Then
W_{\mathfrak{c}}=\mathopen{\{}1,\ldots,30,48,52,53\mathclose{\}}
and the vertices outside this set are highlighted.
Note that for the chain
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}:=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)
with
F^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{49}
and
V^{\prime}=\mathopen{\{}1,\ldots,52\mathclose{\}}
, the reduction operation is trivial in the sense that
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}|\text{r}=\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
due to
W_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}=V^{\prime}
.
Hence, an extension that transforms
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
into
\mathfrak{c}
transforms a chain where reduction is trivial into a chain where this is not the case.

With these transformations, we can now formally define
branching
and
support
.
Let
\beta\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e
where
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I]
and suppose that
\ell^{\prime}\geq 0
is minimal such that
e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}|\ell^{\prime}}
.
We say that
\mathfrak{c}|[\beta]:=\mathfrak{c}|\ell^{\prime}|\beta|\text{r}
is the
\beta
-branching
of
\mathfrak{c}
and that the chain
\mathfrak{c}|e:=\mathfrak{c}|[\beta]|\mathord{-}
, which only depends on
e
and
\mathfrak{c}
, is the
e
-support
in
\mathfrak{c}
.

Suppose that
U\subseteq V
.
For
\psi\colon U\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
and
i\geq 0
, we set
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}^{\sim}(i):=\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},\psi}^{\sim}
and
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}(i):=\lvert\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}^{\sim}\rvert
.
Furthermore, we set
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},U}:=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},U}
.

Finally, we choose the collection of chains
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)
where we are interested in
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}
for
\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
We call a collection
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
of chains
admissible
if it satisfies the following properties.

(i)

(\mathcal{F},V_{\mathcal{F}},f)\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
.

(ii)

For all
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
where
F
has length
\ell
and all
1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
, we have
\mathfrak{c}|\ell^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
.

(iii)

For all
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
where
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
, and all
\beta\colon f\hookrightarrow e
where
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I]
such that
e\notin\mathcal{F}_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq\ell-1
, we have
\mathfrak{c}|\beta|\text{r}\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
.

Every arbitrary intersection of admissible collections of chains is also admissible.
Hence, there exists an admissible collection of chains that is minimal with respect to inclusion.
We choose the collection
\mathfrak{C}
of chains
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)
with
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
where we are interested in
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}
for all
\psi\colon I\rightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
and
i\geq 0
as this minimal admissible collection.
For our arguments, it is crucial that when considering the chains
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}
, the template
(\mathcal{C},I)
is not too large and that we do not end up with too many random processes
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}(0),\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}(1),\ldots
where
\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
(note that we enforce no bound for the length of the sequence
F
).
Lemma
8.3
below provides suitable bounds for the sizes of the vertex set
V
which in turn yields a suitable bound for the number of such random processes (see Lemma
8.17
).
Lemmas
8.4
and
8.5
state simple useful properties of chains
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathfrak{C}
that are almost immediate from the definition of
\mathfrak{C}
.

Lemma 8.1
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)
is a chain and let
(\mathcal{A},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},I)
.
Then,
\rho_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
F
has length
\ell\geq 1
and that
\mathcal{A}
is an induced subgraph of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.
Suppose that
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
.
For
1\leq i\leq\ell
, let
V_{i}:=V\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}
.
Let
f_{1}:=I
and for
2\leq i\leq\ell
, let
f_{i}\in\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\cap\mathcal{F}_{i}
.
For
1\leq i\leq\ell
, let
U_{i}:=(V_{\mathcal{A}}\cup f_{i})\cap V_{i}
and
\mathcal{A}_{i}:=\mathcal{F}_{i}[U_{i}]
.
Note that since
(\mathcal{F}_{i},f_{i})
is balanced, we have
\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{i},f_{i}}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
.
Since
F
is a vertex-separated loose path, we have

V_{\mathcal{A}}\setminus I=\bigcup_{1\leq i\leq\ell}U_{i}\setminus f_{i}.

and
(U_{i}\setminus f_{i})\cap(U_{j}\setminus f_{j})=\emptyset
for all
1\leq i<j\leq\ell
.
This entails
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert=\sum_{1\leq i\leq\ell}\lvert U_{i}\rvert-\lvert f_{i}\rvert
.
Furthermore,

\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{A}[I]=\bigcup_{1\leq i\leq\ell}\mathcal{F}_{i}[V_{\mathcal{A}}\cap V_{i}]\setminus\mathcal{F}_{i}[f_{i}]\subseteq\bigcup_{1\leq i\leq\ell}\mathcal{A}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{i}[f_{i}].

Similarly as above, since
(\mathcal{A}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{i}[f_{i}])\cap(\mathcal{A}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{j}[f_{j}])=\emptyset
for all
1\leq i<j\leq\ell
, this entails
\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}[I]\rvert\leq\sum_{1\leq i\leq\ell}\lvert\mathcal{A}_{i}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}_{i}[f_{i}]\rvert
.
Thus, we obtain

\rho_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq\frac{\sum_{1\leq i\leq\ell}\lvert\mathcal{A}_{i}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}_{i}[f_{i}]\rvert}{\sum_{1\leq i\leq\ell}\lvert U_{i}\rvert-\lvert f_{i}\rvert}=\frac{\sum_{1\leq i\leq\ell}\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{i},f_{i}}(\lvert U_{i}\rvert-\lvert f_{i}\rvert)}{\sum_{1\leq i\leq\ell}\lvert U_{i}\rvert-\lvert f_{i}\rvert}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.2
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)
is a chain with
\lvert V\rvert\geq 1/\varepsilon^{3}
.
Then,
W_{\mathfrak{c}}\neq V
.

Proof.

Suppose that
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
.
We show that for
W:=V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}+\mathcal{F}_{\ell}}
, as a consequence of Lemma
8.1
, we have
\rho_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},W}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}
.
Then, we obtain
W_{\mathfrak{c}}\neq V
by choice of
W_{\mathfrak{c}}
.

Let us turn to the details.
We have

\lvert\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[W]\rvert\leq\lvert\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I]\rvert

and

\lvert V\rvert-\lvert W\rvert\geq\lvert V\rvert-\lvert I\rvert-2m=\bigg\lparen 1-\frac{2m}{\lvert V\rvert-k}\bigg\rparen(\lvert V\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)\geq\bigg\lparen 1-\frac{2m}{\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{3}}-k}\bigg\rparen(\lvert V\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)\geq\frac{\lvert V\rvert-\lvert I\rvert}{1+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}}}.

With Lemma
8.1
, this yields

\rho_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},W}\leq\bigg\lparen 1+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg\rparen\frac{\lvert\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I]\rvert}{\lvert V\rvert-\lvert I\rvert}\leq\bigg\lparen 1+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg\rparen\rho_{\mathcal{F}}=\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}
.
Then,
\lvert V\rvert\leq 1/\varepsilon^{3}
.

Proof.

Consider the collection
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
of all chains
(F,V,I)
with
\lvert V\rvert\leq 1/\varepsilon^{3}
.
As a consequence of Lemma
8.2
, this collection is admissible, so we have
\mathfrak{C}\subseteq\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathfrak{C}
.
Then,
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}
.

Proof.

Consider the collection
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
of all chains
\mathfrak{c}
with
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}
.
By choice of
\mathfrak{C}
, if
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
is admissible, then
\mathfrak{C}\subseteq\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
, so it suffices to show that
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
is admissible.

For all
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
\mathfrak{c}:=(\mathcal{F},V_{\mathcal{F}},f)
, we have
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}
.
Consider
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
where
F
has length
\ell
and let
1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)=\mathfrak{c}|\ell^{\prime}
, let
V^{\prime\prime}:=V_{\mathcal{F}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}+\ldots+\mathcal{F}_{\ell}}\cap V
and let
\mathcal{C}:=\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}
.
Since for all
(V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cup V_{\mathcal{F}_{\ell^{\prime}}})\cap V\subseteq W\subseteq V^{\prime}
, we have
\rho_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime},W}=\rho_{\mathcal{C},W\cup V^{\prime\prime}}
, from
W_{\mathfrak{c}}=V
, we obtain
W_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}=V^{\prime}
.
Hence, we have
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}|\text{r}=\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
and thus
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
.
Finally, since for all chains
\mathfrak{c}
, we have
\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}=\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}|\text{r}
, we conclude that
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
is admissible.
∎

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}
where
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
.
Then,
\lvert\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I]\rvert\geq\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert-1
and hence
\ell\geq 1
.

Proof.

Consider the collection
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
of all chains
(F,V,I)
where
F^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
for some
\ell\geq 1
such that
V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\subseteq V
.
For all
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
, we have
\lvert\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}[I^{\prime}]\rvert\geq\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert-1
.
Furthermore,
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
is admissible, so we have
\mathfrak{C}\subseteq\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
.
∎

8.2.
Branching and support

In this section, we follow the argumentation in the beginning of Section
8
to obtain Lemma
8.15
where use the branching and support constructions to estimate the expected number of embeddings of a chain template lost when removing the next randomly chosen copy of
\mathcal{F}
.
As preparation for the proof of Lemma
8.15
we first consider templates
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},V_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}})
that correspond to truncation and reduction transformations introduced above in the sense that
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
is the transformation of the chain
\mathfrak{c}
.
For such templates, we show that we can control the number of embeddings based on control over balanced extensions (see Lemma
8.11
, Lemma
8.12
and Lemma
8.14
).
To this end, we first state Lemma
8.6
that quantifies the number of embeddings that avoid a given small subset of
V_{\mathcal{H}}
, which will be helpful in the following situations.
Suppose that
(\mathcal{A},I)
is a template and that
J\subseteq I
is a subset such that for all
e\in\mathcal{A}
with
e\cap J\neq\emptyset
, we have
e\in\mathcal{A}[I]
and suppose that
\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
Let
\psi^{\prime}:=\psi|_{I\setminus J}
.
Then, the number
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}
of embeddings of
(\mathcal{A},I)
that extend
\psi
is equal to the number of embeddings
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A}-J,\psi^{\prime}}^{\sim}
of
(\mathcal{A}-J,I\setminus J)
that extend
\psi^{\prime}
and additionally avoid
\psi(J)
in the sense that
\varphi(V_{\mathcal{A}-J})\cap\psi(J)=\emptyset
.
We introduce the following notation.
For a template
(\mathcal{A},I)
,
\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
and
W\subseteq V_{\mathcal{H}}\setminus\psi(I)
, let

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},I}^{\sim,W}:=\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A},I}^{\sim}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(V_{\mathcal{A}})\cap W=\emptyset\mathclose{\}}\quad\text{and}\quad\Phi_{\mathcal{A},I}^{W}:=\lvert\Phi_{\mathcal{A},I}^{\sim,W}\rvert.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
0\leq i\leq i^{\star}
and
\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i<\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\mathclose{\}}
.
Suppose that
(\mathcal{A},I)
is a template with
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert\leq 1/\varepsilon^{4}
and
\rho_{\mathcal{B},I}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}
for all
(\mathcal{B},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
.
Let
\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
and
W\subseteq V_{\mathcal{H}}\setminus\psi(I)
with
\lvert W\rvert\leq 1/\varepsilon^{3}
.
Then,

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}-\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}^{W}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}\zeta^{3/2}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}.

Proof.

For
v\in V_{\mathcal{A}}\setminus I
and
w\in W
, let
\psi_{v}^{w}\colon I\cup\mathopen{\{}v\mathclose{\}}\hookrightarrow\psi(I)\cup\mathopen{\{}w\mathclose{\}}
with
\psi_{v}^{w}|_{I}=\psi
.
We have

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}-\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}^{W}\leq\sum_{v\in V_{\mathcal{A}}\setminus I}\sum_{w\in W}\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}^{\sim}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(v)=w\mathclose{\}}\rvert=\sum_{v\in V_{\mathcal{A}}\setminus I}\sum_{w\in W}\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi_{v}^{w}}.

Hence, it suffices to show that for all
v\in V_{\mathcal{A}}\setminus I
and
w\in W
, we have
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi_{v}^{w}}\leq\zeta^{5/3}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}
.
We show that this is a consequence of Lemma
7.16
.

To this end, suppose that
v\in V_{\mathcal{A}}\setminus I
and
w\in W
.
For all subtemplates
(\mathcal{B},I)\subseteq(\mathcal{A},I)
with
v\in V_{\mathcal{B}}
, using the fact that
\zeta^{-1}\leq n^{1/2}\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}/2}
and Lemma
7.5
, we have

\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{B},I}=(n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{B},I}})^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{B}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert}\geq(n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}})^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{B}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert}\geq n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}}\geq(n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+8\varepsilon^{2}})^{1/8}\zeta^{-7/4}\geq\zeta^{-7/4}.

Thus, Lemma
7.16
entails

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi_{v}^{w}}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I\cup\mathopen{\{}v\mathclose{\}}}}\zeta^{7/4}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq\zeta^{5/3}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.7
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}
is a subsequence of a vertex-separated loose path.
Then, there exist edges
e_{1}\in\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
e_{2}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
with
V_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\subseteq e_{1}\cap e_{2}
.

Proof.

Consider a vertex-separated loose path
B=\mathcal{B}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{B}_{\ell}
that has
\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}
as a subsequence.
Let
1\leq i<j\leq\ell
such that
\mathcal{B}_{i}=\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{j}=\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
Let
e_{1}
denote the unique edge in
\mathcal{B}_{i}\cap\mathcal{B}_{i+1}
and let
e_{2}
denote the unique edge in
\mathcal{B}_{j-1}\cap\mathcal{B}_{j}
.
Then,

V_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}=V_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\cap V_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{B}_{1}+\ldots+\mathcal{B}_{i}}\cap V_{\mathcal{B}_{i+1}+\ldots+\mathcal{B}_{\ell}}=e_{1}

and similarly

V_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}=V_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\cap V_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{B}_{1}+\ldots+\mathcal{B}_{j-1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{B}_{j}+\ldots+\mathcal{B}_{\ell}}=e_{2},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.8
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}
is a subsequence of a vertex-separated loose path of copies of
\mathcal{F}
.
Let
I:=V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}
.
Then,
\lvert I\rvert=k
or
\lvert I\rvert\leq k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
.
Hence, if
I\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
for some
k
-graph
\mathcal{A}
that has exactly one edge and no isolated vertices, then
\rho_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{F}_{1}\cap\mathcal{F}_{2}\neq\emptyset
, then
\lvert I\rvert=k
and hence the statement follows.
Thus, we may assume that
\mathcal{F}_{1}\cap\mathcal{F}_{2}=\emptyset
.
Consider a vertex-separated loose path
F^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}^{\prime}
of copies of
\mathcal{F}
that has
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}
as a subsequence.
Let
1\leq i<j\leq\ell
such that
\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}_{2}
.
Since
\mathcal{F}_{1}\cap\mathcal{F}_{2}=\emptyset
, we have
j\geq i+2
.
Choose
f_{-},f_{+}\in\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\prime}
such that
f_{-}
is the unique edge in
\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\prime}
and such that
f_{+}
is the unique edge in
\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{F}_{i+2}^{\prime}
.
Then,

V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}=V_{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{j}^{\prime}}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\prime}+\ldots+\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\prime}+\ldots+\mathcal{F}_{\ell}^{\prime}}=f_{-}

and similarly

V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}=V_{\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{j}^{\prime}}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\prime}+\ldots+\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\prime}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{i+2}^{\prime}+\ldots+\mathcal{F}_{\ell}^{\prime}}=f_{+}.

Hence,
V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\subseteq f_{-}\cap f_{+}
.
Thus, it suffices to show that
\lvert f_{-}\cap f_{+}\rvert\leq k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
.
This follows from the fact that
(\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\prime},f_{-})
is balanced.
To see this, consider the template
(\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\prime}[f_{-}\cup f_{+}],f_{-})
.
Then,

\rho_{\mathcal{F}}\geq\rho_{\mathcal{F}_{i+1}^{\prime}[f_{-}\cup f_{+}],f_{-}}\geq\frac{1}{\lvert f_{-}\cup f_{+}\rvert-\lvert f_{-}\rvert}=\frac{1}{k-\lvert f_{-}\cap f_{+}\rvert}

and hence
\lvert f_{-}\cap f_{+}\rvert\leq k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
.
∎

Lemma 8.9
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}
is a subsequence of a vertex-separated loose path of copies of
\mathcal{F}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{A}
is a subgraph of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
or
\mathcal{F}_{2}
.
Let
I:=V_{\mathcal{A}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}
.
Then,
\rho_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{F}_{2},\mathcal{F}_{1}
is also a subsequence of a vertex-separated loose path of copies of
\mathcal{F}
, we may assume that
\mathcal{A}
is a subgraph of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
.
Furthermore, we may assume that
\mathcal{A}
is an induced subgraph of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
.
By Lemma
8.7
, we may fix an edge
f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}
with
V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\subseteq f_{1}
.
If
f_{1}\not\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\mathcal{A}[I]=\emptyset
and thus, using the fact that
(\mathcal{F}_{1},f_{1})
is balanced, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}[I]\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert\mathcal{F}_{1}[V_{\mathcal{A}}]\rvert\leq\lvert\mathcal{F}_{1}[V_{\mathcal{A}}\cup f_{1}]\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{F}_{1}[f_{1}]\rvert=\rho_{\mathcal{F}_{1}[V_{\mathcal{A}}\cup f_{1}],f_{1}}(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\cup f_{1}\rvert-\lvert f_{1}\rvert)

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\cup f_{1}\rvert-\lvert f_{1}\rvert)=\rho_{\mathcal{F}}\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\setminus f_{1}\rvert\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert).

If
f_{1}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
I=V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}
, so using the fact that
(\mathcal{F}_{1},f_{1})
is balanced and Lemma
8.8
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}[I]\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}[f_{1}]\rvert+\lvert\mathcal{F}_{1}[f_{1}]\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{F}_{1}[I]\rvert=\rho_{\mathcal{A},f_{1}}(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert f_{1}\rvert)+\rho_{\mathcal{F}_{1}[f_{1}],I}(\lvert f_{1}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert f_{1}\rvert)+\rho_{\mathcal{F}}(\lvert f_{1}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)=\rho_{\mathcal{F}}(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.10
.

Let
0\leq i\leq i^{\star}
and
\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i<\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\mathclose{\}}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}
is a subsequence of a vertex-separated loose path of copies of
\mathcal{F}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{A}
is a subgraph of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
or
\mathcal{F}_{2}
.
Let
I:=V_{\mathcal{A}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}
.
Let
\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
Then,

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\varepsilon^{-1}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}.

Proof.

We use induction on
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert
to show that

(8.4)

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm 2(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}.

If
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert=0
, then
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}=1=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}
.
Let
\ell\geq 1
and suppose that (
8.4
) holds if
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert\leq\ell-1
.
Suppose that
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert=\ell
.
From Lemma
8.9
, we obtain
\rho_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
.
Suppose that among all subsets
I\subseteq U^{\prime}\subsetneq V_{\mathcal{A}}
with
\rho_{\mathcal{A},U^{\prime}}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}
, the set
U
has maximal size.
By Lemma
7.3
, the extension
(\mathcal{A},U)
is balanced.
We have

(8.5)

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}=\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}.

We use the estimate for
\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}
provided by the induction hypothesis and for
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}^{\sim}
, we estimate
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}
using the balancedness of
(\mathcal{A},U)
to conclude that
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}
is bounded as desired.

Let us turn to the details.
Since
\zeta^{-2}\leq n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}}
, for all
j\leq i
, we have

\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},U}(j)=(n\hat{p}(j)^{\rho_{\mathcal{A},U}})^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert U\rvert}\geq(n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert U\rvert}\geq\zeta^{-2(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert U\rvert)}\geq\zeta^{-2}>\zeta^{-\delta^{1/2}}.

Hence
i<i_{\mathcal{A},U}^{\delta^{1/2}}
, and thus for all
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}
, we have
\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\varphi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},U}
.
Since by induction hypothesis, we have
\Phi_{\mathcal{A}[U],\psi}=(1\pm 2(\lvert U\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}
, returning to (
8.5
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}=(1\pm 2(\lvert U\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)\zeta^{\delta})(1\pm\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},U}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}[U],I}=(1\pm 2(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert)\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.11
.

Let
0\leq i\leq i^{\star}
and
\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i<\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\mathclose{\}}
.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}
is a chain where
F
has length
\ell
.
Let
0\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
and suppose that
(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)=\mathfrak{c}|\ell^{\prime}
.
Let
\psi\colon V^{\prime}\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
Then,
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\varepsilon^{-5k}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

For
0\leq\ell_{0}\leq\ell
, let

g_{\ell_{0}}:=\lvert\mathopen{\{}\ell_{0}\leq\ell_{1}\leq\ell-1\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}|\ell_{1}}\neq\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}|\ell_{1}+1}\mathclose{\}}\rvert.

We use induction on
\ell-\ell^{\prime}
to show that

(8.6)

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm 4g_{\ell^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{-1}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C},V^{\prime}}.

By Lemma
8.3
, we have
\lvert V\rvert\leq\varepsilon^{-3}
, hence
\lvert\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\rvert\leq\varepsilon^{-3k}
and thus
g_{\ell^{\prime}}\leq\varepsilon^{-3}+\varepsilon^{-3k}\leq\varepsilon^{-4k}
, so it suffices to obtain (
8.6
).

Let us proceed with the proof by induction.
If
\ell-\ell^{\prime}=0
, then
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}=1=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C},V^{\prime}}
.
Let
q\geq 1
and suppose that (
8.6
) holds whenever
\ell-\ell^{\prime}\leq q-1
.
Suppose that
\ell-\ell^{\prime}=q
.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)=\mathfrak{c}|\ell^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}=(F^{\prime\prime},V^{\prime\prime},I)=\mathfrak{c}|\ell^{\prime}+1
.
If
\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}
, then (
8.6
) follows by induction hypothesis, so we may assume
\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}}\neq\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}
and hence
g_{\ell^{\prime}+1}=g_{\ell^{\prime}}-1
.
We have

(8.7)

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}=\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime},\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}.

We use Lemma
8.10
to estimate
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime},\psi}
and for
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime},\psi}^{\sim}
, we use the estimate for
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}
provided by the induction hypothesis to conclude that
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}
can be estimated as desired.

Let us turn to the details.
Let
\mathcal{A}:=\mathcal{F}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}[V\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}}]
and
J:=V_{\mathcal{A}}\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{\ell^{\prime}}}
.
Note that
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},J}=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime},V^{\prime}}
.
Lemma
8.9
allows us to apply Lemma
8.6
such that using Lemma
8.10
, we obtain

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime},\psi}=\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi|_{J}}^{\psi(V^{\prime}\setminus J)}=_{\mathcal{X}}\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi|_{J}}\pm\zeta^{3/2}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},J}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm 2\varepsilon^{-1}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},J}=(1\pm 2\varepsilon^{-1}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime},V^{\prime}}.

Furthermore, by induction hypothesis, for all
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime},\psi}^{\sim}
, we have

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm 4g_{\ell^{\prime}+1}\varepsilon^{-1}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C},V^{\prime\prime}}=(1\pm 4(g_{\ell^{\prime}}-1)\varepsilon^{-1}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C},V^{\prime\prime}}.

Thus, returning to (
8.7
), we conclude that

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm 2\varepsilon^{-1}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime},V^{\prime}}\cdot(1\pm 4(g_{\ell^{\prime}}-1)\varepsilon^{-1}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C},V^{\prime\prime}}=(1\pm 4g_{\ell^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{-1}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C},V^{\prime}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.12
.

Let
0\leq i\leq i^{\star}
and
\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i<\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\mathclose{\}}
.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}
is the
\beta
-extension of a chain in
\mathfrak{C}
for some
\beta
and let
(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)=\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}
.
Let
\psi\colon V^{\prime}\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
Then,
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}=(1\pm\varepsilon^{-4}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)
where
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
.
By definition of
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}:=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)
, there exists a sequence of chains
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V_{0},I),\ldots,(F,V_{t},I)=\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
with
V_{0}\supseteq\ldots\supseteq V_{t}
such that for all
1\leq s\leq t
, the set
V_{s}
is a subset of
V_{s-1}
of maximal size chosen from all subsets
(V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cup V_{\mathcal{F}_{\ell}})\cap V_{s-1}\subseteq W\subsetneq V_{s-1}
with
\rho_{\mathcal{C}_{(F,V_{s-1},I)},W}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}
.

For
0\leq s\leq t
, let
\mathcal{C}_{s}:=\mathcal{C}_{(F,V_{s},I)}
.
Using induction on
s
, we show that for all
0\leq s\leq t
and
\psi_{s}\colon V_{s}\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
, we have

(8.8)

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi_{s}}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm 2s\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V_{s}}.

By Lemma
8.3
, we have
\lvert V\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon^{-3}
and hence
2t\leq\varepsilon^{-4}
, so this is sufficient.

Let us proceed with the proof by induction.
If
s=0
, then, for all
\psi_{s}\colon V_{s}\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
, we have
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi_{s}}=1=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V_{s}}
.
Let
q\geq 1
and suppose that (
8.8
) holds whenever
s\leq q-1
.
Suppose that
s=q
and let
\psi_{s}\colon V_{s}\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
We have

(8.9)

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi_{s}}=\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},\psi_{s}}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}.

By Lemma
7.3
, the extension
(\mathcal{C}_{s-1},V_{s})
is balanced, so we may estimate
\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},\psi_{s}}
based on balancedness, while for
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},\psi_{s}}
the induction hypothesis provides an estimate for
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}
.

Let us turn to the details.
Using the fact that
\zeta^{-1}\leq n^{1/2}\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}/2}
and Lemma
7.5
, for all
j\leq i
, we obtain

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},V_{s}}(j)

\displaystyle=(n\hat{p}(j)^{\rho_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},V_{s}}})^{\lvert V_{s-1}\rvert-\lvert V_{s}\rvert}\geq(n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}})^{\lvert V_{s-1}\rvert-\lvert V_{s}\rvert}\geq n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}}\geq(n\hat{p}^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+2\varepsilon^{2}})^{1/2}\zeta^{-1}

\displaystyle\geq\zeta^{-1}>\zeta^{-\delta^{1/2}}.

Hence
i<i_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},V_{s}}^{\delta^{1/2}}
and thus
\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},\psi_{s}}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},V_{s}}
.
Furthermore, for all
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},\psi_{s}}
, by induction hypothesis we have
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm 2(s-1)\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V_{s-1}}
, so returning to (
8.9
), we conclude that

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi_{s}}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{C}_{s-1},V_{s}}\cdot(1\pm 2(s-1)\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V_{s-1}}=(1\pm 2s\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V_{s}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.13
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}
is the
\beta
-extension of a chain in
\mathfrak{C}
for some
\beta
and let
(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)=\mathfrak{c}|\text{r}
.
Let
(\mathcal{A},V^{\prime})\subseteq(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},V^{\prime})

Then,
\rho_{\mathcal{A},V^{\prime}}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)
where
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
.
By definition of
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}:=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)
, there exists a sequence of chains
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V_{0},I),\ldots,(F,V_{t},I)=\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
with
V_{0}\supseteq\ldots\supseteq V_{t}
such that for all
1\leq s\leq t
, the set
V_{s}
is a subset of
V_{s-1}
of maximal size chosen from all subsets
(V_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\cup V_{\mathcal{F}_{\ell}})\cap V_{s-1}\subseteq W\subsetneq V_{s-1}
with
\rho_{\mathcal{C}_{(F,V_{s-1},I)},W}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}
.

For
0\leq s\leq t
, let
\mathcal{C}_{s}:=\mathcal{C}_{(F,V_{s},I)}
and
\mathcal{A}_{s}:=\mathcal{A}[V_{s}\cap V_{\mathcal{A}}]
and for
0\leq s\leq t-1
, let
\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{A}_{s}+\mathcal{C}_{s+1}
.
For
0\leq s\leq t-1
, consider the extensions
(\mathcal{A}_{s},V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}})
and
(\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime},V_{s+1})
.
We have

V_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\setminus V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}=(V_{s}\cap V_{\mathcal{A}})\setminus(V_{s+1}\cap V_{\mathcal{A}})=(V_{s}\cap V_{\mathcal{A}})\setminus V_{s+1}=(V_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\cup V_{s+1})\setminus V_{s+1}=V_{\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime}}\setminus V_{s+1}

and hence
\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\rvert-\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}\rvert=\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime}}\rvert-\lvert V_{s+1}\rvert
.
Furthermore, we have
\mathcal{C}_{s+1}\cap\mathcal{A}_{s}=\mathcal{A}_{s}[V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}]
and
\mathcal{A}_{s}[V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}]\cup\mathcal{C}_{s+1}=\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime}[V_{s+1}]
, hence

\mathcal{A}_{s}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{s}[V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}]=\mathcal{A}_{s}\setminus(\mathcal{A}_{s}[V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}]\cup\mathcal{C}_{s+1})=\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime}\setminus(\mathcal{A}_{s}[V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}]\cup\mathcal{C}_{s+1})=\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime}[V_{s+1}]

and thus
\lvert\mathcal{A}_{s}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}_{s}[V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}]\rvert=\lvert\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime}[V_{s+1}]\rvert
.
In particular, this yields
\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{s},V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}}=\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime},V_{s+1}}
.
Since
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}
implies
\mathcal{A}_{s}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[V_{s}]=\mathcal{C}_{s}
, we have
(\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime},V_{s+1})\subseteq(\mathcal{C}_{s},V_{s+1})
.
Using Lemma
7.3
, this entails

\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{s},V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}}=\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{s}^{\prime},V_{s+1}}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{C}_{s},V_{s+1}}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}[V^{\prime}]\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\lvert\mathcal{A}_{s}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}_{s+1}\rvert=\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{s},V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}}(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\rvert-\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}\rvert)

\displaystyle\leq(\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2})\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\rvert-\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}_{s+1}}\rvert=(\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2})(\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert V^{\prime}\rvert),

which completes the proof. ∎

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
0\leq i\leq i^{\star}
and
\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i<\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\mathclose{\}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}
and
e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I]
.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)=\mathfrak{c}|e
.
Let
\psi\colon V^{\prime}\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
Then,
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}=(1\pm\varepsilon^{-6k}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Consider an arbitrary
\beta\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e
where
f\in\mathcal{F}
.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}=(F^{\prime\prime},V^{\prime\prime},I)=\mathfrak{c}|[\beta]
.
Furthermore, suppose that
\ell\geq 1
is minimal with
e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}|\ell}
and suppose that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+}=(F^{\prime}_{+},V^{\prime}_{+},I):=\mathfrak{c}|{\ell},\quad\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}_{+}=(F^{\prime\prime}_{+},V^{\prime\prime}_{+},I):=\mathfrak{c}|\ell|\beta.

We have

(8.10)

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}=\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}.

We use Lemma
8.12
to estimate
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}
and for
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}^{\sim}
, we use Lemma
8.11
to estimate
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}
.

Let us turn to the details.
First, consider
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}
.
Choose an arbitrary injection
\psi^{\prime}\colon V^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
with
\psi^{\prime}|_{V^{\prime}}=\psi
.
With Lemma
8.12
, since
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}_{+},V^{\prime\prime}}=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},V^{\prime}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}_{+},\psi^{\prime}}+\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}-\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}^{\psi^{\prime}(V^{\prime\prime}\setminus V^{\prime})}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\varepsilon^{-4}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}_{+},V^{\prime\prime}}+\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}-\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}^{\psi^{\prime}(V^{\prime\prime}\setminus V^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=(1\pm\varepsilon^{-4}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},V^{\prime}}+\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}-\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}^{\psi^{\prime}(V^{\prime\prime}\setminus V^{\prime})}.

To bound
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}-\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}^{\psi^{\prime}(V^{\prime\prime}\setminus V^{\prime})}
, we employ Lemma
8.6
which we may apply as a consequence of Lemma
8.13
.
To this end, recall that in Section
8.1
, to define the
\beta
-extension of
\mathfrak{c}
, we fixed a copy
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}
of
\mathcal{F}
.
For all
(\mathcal{A},V^{\prime})\subseteq(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+}},V^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\beta}
, the template
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},V^{\prime\prime})
is a subtemplate of
(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}_{+}},V^{\prime\prime})
and we have
\rho_{\mathcal{A},V^{\prime}}=\rho_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime},V^{\prime\prime}}
, so Lemma
8.13
entails
\rho_{\mathcal{A},V^{\prime}}\leq\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon^{2}
.
Hence, we may apply Lemma
8.6
to obtain

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}-\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}^{\psi^{\prime}(V^{\prime\prime}\setminus V^{\prime})}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}\zeta^{3/2}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},V^{\prime}}.

Thus,

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\varepsilon^{-5}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},V^{\prime}}.

Next, fix
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},\psi}^{\sim}
and consider
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}
.
Then, Lemma
8.11
entails

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\varepsilon^{-5k}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}_{+}}.

Thus, returning to (
8.10
), we conclude that

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\varepsilon^{-5}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{+},V^{\prime}}\cdot(1\pm\varepsilon^{-5k}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}_{+}}=(1\pm\varepsilon^{-6k}\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.15
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}
and let
e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I]
.
Let
0\leq i\leq i^{\star}
and

\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i<\tau_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{F}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\mathclose{\}}\cap\mathopen{\{}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}\leq 2\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}\mathclose{\}}\cap\mathopen{\{}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}\leq 2\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c}|e,I}\mathclose{\}}.

Then,

\mathbb{E}_{i}\mathopen{[}\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(e)\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(i+1)\mathclose{\}}\rvert\mathclose{]}=_{\mathcal{X}}\bigg\lparen\sum_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\beta\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e}\frac{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|[\beta],\psi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}}{\aut(\mathcal{F})H^{*}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\bigg\rparen\pm\zeta^{1+\delta/2}\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}}{H}.

Proof.

Lemma
7.18
entails

(8.11)

\mathbb{E}_{i}\mathopen{[}\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(e)\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(i+1)\mathclose{\}}\rvert\mathclose{]}=\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}}\frac{d_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}(\varphi(e))}{H^{*}}=\sum_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\beta\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e}\frac{\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}}{\aut(\mathcal{F})H^{*}}.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{c}|e=(F^{\prime},V^{\prime},I)
.
For
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
\beta\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e
, using Lemma
8.14
and the fact that
\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}=_{\mathcal{X}}(1\pm\delta^{-1}\zeta)\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}
holds for all
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}^{\sim}
, Lemma
7.21
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}

\displaystyle=_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{1}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\Big\lparen\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}\Big\rparen\Big\lparen\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\varphi}\Big\rparen\pm\delta^{-2}\zeta^{1+\delta}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\Big\lparen\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}\Big\rparen\pm\delta^{-2}\zeta^{1+\delta}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}.

Since Lemma
8.6
entails

\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}=_{\mathcal{X}}\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}(\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\varphi\circ\beta}^{\varphi(V^{\prime}\setminus e)}\pm\zeta^{3/2}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f})=\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|[\beta],\psi}\pm\zeta^{3/2}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi},

we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi\circ\beta}

\displaystyle=_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|[\beta],\psi}}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\pm\delta^{-2}\zeta^{1+\delta}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}\pm\zeta^{3/2}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}

\displaystyle=_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|[\beta],\psi}}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\pm\delta^{-3}\zeta^{1+\delta}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},V^{\prime}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c}|e,I}\pm\zeta^{4/3}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|[\beta],\psi}}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\pm\delta^{-4}\zeta^{1+\delta}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}.

Combining this with (
8.11
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{i}\mathopen{[}\lvert\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi^{\sim}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(e)\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(i+1)\mathclose{\}}\rvert\mathclose{]}=_{\mathcal{X}}\bigg\lparen\sum_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\beta\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e}\frac{\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|[\beta],\psi}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}}{\aut(\mathcal{F})H^{*}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|e,\psi}}\bigg\rparen\pm\frac{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert k!\,\zeta^{1+\delta}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}}{\delta^{4}\aut(\mathcal{F})H^{*}}.

Since Lemma
7.7
yields

\frac{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert k!\,\zeta^{1+\delta}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}}{\delta^{4}\aut(\mathcal{F})H^{*}}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert k!\,\zeta^{1+\delta}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}}{\delta^{5}\aut(\mathcal{F})\hat{h}^{*}}=\frac{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert k!\,\zeta^{1+\delta}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}}{\delta^{5}n^{k}\hat{p}}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}\zeta^{1+\delta/2}\frac{\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}}{H},

this completes the proof.
∎

8.3.
Tracking chains

Suppose that
0\leq i\leq i^{\star}
, consider a chain
\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C}
with
F=\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
and let
\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
We do not directly show that the number of embeddings
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}
is typically close to a deterministic trajectory.
Instead, we define

\mathcal{G}_{\mathfrak{c}}:=\mathcal{F}_{\ell}[V\cap V_{\mathcal{F}_{\ell}}]\quad\text{and}\quad J_{\mathfrak{c}}:=\begin{cases}I&\text{if\penalty\ $\ell=1$};\\
V_{\mathcal{F}_{\ell-1}}\cap V_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathfrak{c}}}&\text{if\penalty\ $\ell\geq 2$}\end{cases}

and show that
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}
is typically close to
\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathfrak{c}},J_{\mathfrak{c}}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|\mathord{-},\psi}
which given
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|\mathord{-},\psi}
is the random quantity our deterministic heuristic estimates for embeddings suggest for

\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{c}|\mathord{-},\psi}^{\sim}}\Phi_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathfrak{c}},\varphi|_{J_{\mathfrak{c}}}}\approx\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}.

To this end, let

\hat{\Phi}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}(i):=\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathfrak{c}},J_{\mathfrak{c}}}\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}|\mathord{-}},\psi}\quad\text{and}\quad X_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}(i):=\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},\psi}-\hat{\Phi}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}.

Our analysis of
\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}
crucially relies on Lemma
8.15
.
There, a sum of numbers of embeddings of branchings of
\mathfrak{c}
is a key quantity which motivates the following definition.
For
e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I]
, the
e
-branching family
of
\mathfrak{c}
is

\mathfrak{B}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{e}:=\mathopen{\{}\mathfrak{b}\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\text{$\mathfrak{b}$ is the\penalty\ $\beta$-branching of\penalty\ $\mathfrak{c}$ for some\penalty\ $\beta\colon f\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}e$ where\penalty\ $f\in\mathcal{F}$}\mathclose{\}}.

We define the stopping times

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathfrak{C}}
