The upper bound in (
D.1
) entails
W_{\mathcal{H}^{*}(0)}^{1}(U)\leq n^{\varepsilon^{3}}\cdot k^{k^{\prime}}
and for all
\ell\geq 1
furthermore
W_{\mathcal{H}^{*}(0)}^{\ell+1}(U)\leq W_{\mathcal{H}^{*}(0)}^{\ell}(U)\cdot n^{\varepsilon^{3}}\cdot k^{k^{\prime}}
.
Hence, we inductively obtain
W_{\mathcal{H}^{*}(0)}^{\ell}(U)\leq k^{\ell k^{\prime}}n^{\ell\varepsilon^{3}}
and in particular
W_{\mathcal{H}^{*}(0)}^{4}(U)\leq n^{\varepsilon^{2}}
.
Thus,

\lvert\mathcal{J}\rvert\leq\sum_{U\in\mathcal{R}}\lvert\pi^{-1}(U)\rvert\leq\lvert\mathcal{R}\rvert n^{\varepsilon^{2}}.

As a consequence of Lemma
D.3
, the number of
k^{\prime}
-sets
U\subseteq V_{\mathcal{H}}
that are not suitable is at most
3\cdot n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon^{1/7}}\cdot(4k)^{k^{\prime}}\leq n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon^{1/6}}
.
Hence,
\lvert\mathcal{J}\rvert\geq\lvert\iota(\mathcal{I}^{*})\rvert-n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon^{1/6}}
and thus

\lvert\mathcal{R}\rvert\geq n^{-\varepsilon^{2}}(\lvert\iota(\mathcal{I}^{*})\rvert-n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon^{1/6}}).

Furthermore, if
U\subseteq V_{\mathcal{H}}
is a
k^{\prime}
-set and
e\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
for some
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in\iota^{-1}(U)
, then for all
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\in\iota^{-1}(U)\setminus\mathopen{\{}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\mathclose{\}}
and
f\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
, we have
\lvert e\cap f\rvert\geq k^{\prime}+1
and for all distinct
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\iota^{-1}(U)\setminus\mathopen{\{}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\mathclose{\}}
,
f\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
and
g\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime\prime}
, we have
f\neq g
.
Thus, there exists
k^{\prime}+1\leq k^{\prime\prime}\leq k
and at least
(\lvert\iota^{-1}(U)\rvert-1)/k
distinct edges
f_{1},\ldots,f_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}
with
\lvert e\cap f_{\ell^{\prime}}\rvert=k^{\prime\prime}
for all
1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
.
Now, let
\mathcal{A}
denote a
k^{\prime\prime}
-cherry, let
I\in\mathcal{A}
and fix
\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow e
.
By Lemma
12.1
, we have

\frac{\lvert\iota^{-1}(U)\rvert-1}{k}\leq\ell\leq\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\leq n^{\varepsilon^{3}}

and thus

\lvert\mathcal{I}^{*}\rvert\leq\sum_{U\in\iota(\mathcal{I}^{*})}\lvert\iota^{-1}(U)\rvert\leq\lvert\iota(\mathcal{I}^{*})\rvert n^{\varepsilon^{2}}.

Overall, this yields

(D.2)

\lvert\mathcal{R}\rvert\geq n^{-\varepsilon^{2}}(n^{-\varepsilon^{2}}\lvert\mathcal{I}^{*}\rvert-n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon^{1/6}}),

so it suffices to find an appropriate lower bound for
\mathcal{I}^{*}
.
Similarly as in the proof of Lemma
14.2
, we may again rely on Lemma
13.8
to obtain
H^{*}\leq_{\mathcal{X}}(1+\varepsilon)H/\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert
precisely as in (
14.2
) and then

H\leq_{\mathcal{X}}H-\frac{1}{4\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert}H+\frac{1}{2}\lvert\mathcal{I}^{*}\rvert

precisely as in (
14.3
).
With Lemma
13.8
, precisely as in (
14.4
), this implies
\lvert\mathcal{I}^{*}\rvert\geq_{\mathcal{X}}n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-2\varepsilon^{2}}
.
Combining this with (
D.2
) yields
\lvert\mathcal{R}\rvert\geq_{\mathcal{X}}n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-5\varepsilon^{2}}
.
∎

Lemma D.7
.

Suppose that
X
is a binomial random variable with parameters
n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-5\varepsilon^{2}}
and
n^{-2\varepsilon^{2}}
and let
Y:=(n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-5\varepsilon^{2}}-\lvert\mathcal{R}(i^{\star})\rvert)\vee 0
.
Let

Z:=Y+\sum_{U\in\mathcal{R}(i^{\star})}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{U}}.

Then,
Z
stochastically dominates
X
.

Proof.

We argue similarly as in the proof of Lemma
14.3
.
First, observe that by Lemma
13.2
, whenever
U\in\mathcal{R}(i^{\star})
, for
i:=0
, we have

N_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}^{1}(\mathcal{G}_{U})\leq\sum_{f\in\mathcal{G}_{U}}d_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}(f)\leq n^{\varepsilon^{2}}

and thus

(D.3)

\lvert\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}^{1}(\mathcal{G}_{U})\cup\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}^{1}(\mathcal{G}_{U}^{\prime})\rvert\leq n^{2\varepsilon^{2}}.

Consider distinct
k^{\prime}
-sets
U,U^{\prime}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{H}}
.
By construction of
\mathcal{R}(i^{\star})
, whenever
U,U^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}(i^{\star})
, then

(\mathcal{N}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}(0)}(\mathcal{G}_{U})\cup\mathcal{N}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}(0)}(\mathcal{G}_{U}^{\prime}))\cap(\mathcal{N}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}(0)}(\mathcal{G}_{U^{\prime}})\cup\mathcal{N}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}(0)}(\mathcal{G}_{U^{\prime}}^{\prime}))=\emptyset.

Thus, for all distinct
U_{1},\ldots,U_{\ell}\in\mathcal{R}(i^{\star})
and all
z_{1},\ldots,z_{\ell-1}\in\mathopen{\{}0,1\mathclose{\}}
, from (
D.3
), we obtain

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{U_{\ell}}}=1\mathrel{|}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{U_{\ell^{\prime}}}}=z_{\ell^{\prime}}\text{ for all }1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell\mathclose{]}=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{E}_{U_{\ell}}\mathclose{]}\geq n^{-2\varepsilon^{2}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
D.1
.

The proof is almost exactly the same as for Theorem
13.1
with the key difference that we replace objects and references with the appropriate analogous constructions and arguments form this section.
Define the events

\mathcal{B}:=\mathopen{\{}H(\tau_{\emptyset})\leq n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon}\mathclose{\}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{X}:=\mathopen{\{}i^{\star}<\tau^{\star}\mathclose{\}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{0}.

We need to show that
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{B}\mathclose{]}
is sufficiently small.
Choose
X
,
Y
and
Z
as in Lemma
D.7
.
Lemma
D.6
entails
\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathopen{\{}Y=0\mathclose{\}}
and hence
\mathopen{\{}Y\neq 0\mathclose{\}}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{c}}
.
Thus, from Observation
D.5
and Lemma
D.7
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{\Big\{}\sum_{U\in\mathcal{R}(i^{\star})}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{U}}\leq n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon}\mathclose{\Big\}}\cap\mathcal{B}\subseteq\lparen\mathopen{\{}Z\leq n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon}\mathclose{\}}\cup\mathopen{\{}Y\neq 0\mathclose{\}}\rparen\cap\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathopen{\{}Z\leq n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon}\mathclose{\}}\cup(\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{c}}\cap\mathcal{B})\subseteq\mathopen{\{}Z\leq n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon}\mathclose{\}}\cup\mathopen{\{}\tau^{\star}\leq i^{\star}\mathclose{\}}\cup(\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\mathsf{c}}\cap\mathcal{B}).

By Lemma
13.8
, we have

H(\tau_{\emptyset})\geq_{\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\mathsf{c}}}\varepsilon H(i^{\star})\geq\varepsilon^{2}n^{k}\hat{p}(i^{\star})\geq n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-2\varepsilon^{2}}

and hence
\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\mathsf{c}}\cap\mathcal{B}=\emptyset
.
Thus, using Lemma
13.16
, we obtain

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{B}\mathclose{]}\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}Z\leq n^{k-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}-\varepsilon}\mathclose{]}+\exp(-n^{1/3}).

With Lemma
D.7
and Chernoff’s inequality (see Lemma
14.4
), this completes the proof.
∎

D.5.
Proofs for Theorems
1.6
and
1.7

In this section, we show how to obtain Theorems
1.6
and
1.7
from Theorems
5.1
and
D.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

By definition of
\tau_{\emptyset}
in Section
13
, this is an immediate consequence of Theorem
D.1
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

The argumentation is essentially the same as in the proof of Theorem
1.3
except that we use Theorem
D.1
instead of Theorem
13.1
.

We define the constants
m
,
\varepsilon
,
\delta
,
n
and the
k
-graphs
\mathcal{H}

\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime}
precisely as in the proof of Theorem
1.3
.
Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
denote the event that
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is
(4m,n^{\varepsilon^{4}})
-bounded,
k^{\prime}
-populated and has
n^{k-1/\rho+\varepsilon^{5}}/k!
edges and let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}
denote the event that

\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}:=\mathopen{\{}\lvert\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime}\rvert\leq n^{k-1/\rho+\varepsilon}\mathclose{\}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}:=\mathopen{\{}n^{k-1/\rho-\varepsilon}\leq\lvert\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime}\rvert\mathclose{\}}.

We need to show that

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}\mathclose{]}\geq 1-\exp(-(\log n)^{5/4}).

Since
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}
, we have
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}\mathclose{]}\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}\mathclose{]}
, so it suffices to obtain sufficiently large lower bounds for
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\mathclose{]}
and
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}\mathclose{]}
.
Due to
k^{\prime}=k-1/\rho
, we may apply Theorem
5.1
with
\varepsilon^{5}
playing the role of
\varepsilon
to obtain
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\mathclose{]}\geq 1-\exp(-(\log n)^{4/3})
and Theorem
D.1
shows that
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{|}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\mathclose{]}\geq 1-\exp(-n^{1/4})
.
Using
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}\mathclose{]}=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{|}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\mathclose{]}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\mathclose{]}
, this yields suitable lower bounds for
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\mathclose{]}
and
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{[}\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}\mathclose{]}
.
∎

List of symbols in Sections
6
–
10
and Appendices

–

X_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}(i)=\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},\psi}(i)-\hat{\Phi}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}(i)

X_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}^{e}=\sum_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{e}}\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{b}},\psi}-\sum_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{e}}\hat{\Phi}_{\mathfrak{b},\psi}

Steps

i^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{A},I}=\min\mathopen{\{}i\geq 0\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}(i)\leq\zeta(i)^{-\alpha}\mathclose{\}}

i^{\star}=\frac{(\vartheta-n^{-1/\rho_{\mathcal{F}}+\varepsilon})n^{k}}{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert k!}

Trajectories

\hat{h}^{*}(i)=\frac{n^{m}\hat{p}(i)^{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert}}{\aut(\mathcal{F})}

\hat{p}(i)=\vartheta-\frac{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert k!\,i}{n^{k}}

\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}(i)=n^{\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert}\hat{p}(i)^{\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}[I]\rvert}

\zeta(i)=\frac{n^{\varepsilon^{2}}}{n^{1/2}\hat{p}(i)^{\rho_{\mathcal{F}}/2}}

Constants

\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I}=\alpha_{\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert}

\alpha_{x}=2^{x+1}-2

m=\lvert V_{\mathcal{F}}\rvert

\rho_{\mathcal{A},I}=\frac{\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{A}[I]\rvert}{\lvert V_{\mathcal{A}}\rvert-\lvert I\rvert}

\rho_{\mathcal{F}}=\frac{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert-1}{\lvert V_{\mathcal{F}}\rvert-k}

\vartheta=k!\,\lvert\mathcal{H}(0)\rvert/n^{k}

Random sets

\Phi_{\mathcal{A},I}^{\sim,W}(i)=\mathopen{\{}\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathcal{A},I}^{\sim}(i)\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\varphi(V_{\mathcal{A}})\cap W=\emptyset\mathclose{\}}

\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}^{\sim}(i)=\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}},\psi}^{\sim}(i)

Stopping times

\tau_{\mathscr{B}}=\min\mathopen{\{}i\geq 0\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\neq(1\pm\zeta^{\delta})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}\text{ and }i\leq i^{\delta^{1/2}}_{\mathcal{A},I}\text{for some }(\mathcal{A},I)\in\mathscr{B},\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}\mathclose{\}}

\tau_{\mathfrak{B}}=\min\mathopen{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i\geq 0:&\sum_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{e}}\Phi_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{b}},\psi}\neq\sum_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{e}}\hat{\Phi}_{\mathfrak{b},\psi}\pm\varepsilon^{-\chi_{\mathfrak{B}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{e}}}\zeta\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{b},I}\\
&\quad\text{for some }\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C},e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I],\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}\end{array}\right\}\mathclose{}

\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}=\min\mathopen{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i\geq 0:&\Phi_{\mathcal{A},\psi}\neq(1\pm(\log n)^{\alpha_{\mathcal{A},I}}\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}^{-\delta^{1/2}})\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A},I}\text{ and }i^{\delta^{1/2}}_{\mathcal{A},I}\leq i\leq i^{0}_{\mathcal{A},I}\\
&\quad\text{for some }(\mathcal{A},I)\in\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}\end{array}\right\}\mathclose{}

\tau_{\mathfrak{C}}=\min\mathopen{\{}i\geq 0\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\Phi_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}\neq\hat{\Phi}_{\mathfrak{c},\psi}\pm\delta^{-1}\zeta\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{c},I}\text{ for some }\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C},\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}\mathclose{\}}

\tau_{\mathscr{F}}=\min\mathopen{\{}i\geq 0\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\Phi_{\mathcal{F},\psi}\neq(1\pm\delta^{-1}\zeta)\hat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{F},f}\text{ for some }(\mathcal{F},f)\in\mathscr{F},\psi\colon f\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}\mathclose{\}}

\tau_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}=\min\mathopen{\{}i\geq 0\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}H^{*}\neq(1\pm\zeta^{1+\varepsilon^{3}})\hat{h}^{*}\mathclose{\}}

\tau_{\emptyset}=\min\mathopen{\{}i\geq 0\mathrel{\colonDelimiter}\mathcal{H}^{*}(i)=\emptyset\mathclose{\}}

\tau^{\star}=\tau_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\wedge\tau_{\mathfrak{C}}\wedge\tau_{\mathfrak{B}}

\tilde{\tau}_{\mathfrak{B}}=\min\mathopen{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i\geq 0:&\sum_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{e}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{b},\psi}\neq\sum_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{e}}\hat{\Phi}_{\mathfrak{b},\psi}\pm\delta^{-1/2}\zeta\hat{\varphi}_{\mathfrak{b},I}\\
&\quad\text{for some }\mathfrak{c}=(F,V,I)\in\mathfrak{C},e\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{c}}[I],\psi\colon I\hookrightarrow V_{\mathcal{H}}\end{array}\right\}\mathclose{}

\tilde{\tau}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\star}=\tau_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\wedge\tau_{\mathfrak{C}}\wedge\tilde{\tau}_{\mathfrak{B}}

\tilde{\tau}^{\star}=\tau_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}\wedge\tau_{\mathscr{F}}
